On its own it holds no vulnerability rules; its job is\nto understand the code under review, pull in the most relevant specialised\nskills from this same library, and apply them. This library contains hundreds\nof specialised skills (cryptographic audit, SQL injection, JWT verification,\nsecrets in source, deserialization, access control, and many more) whose\nindividual descriptions are often too narrow to self-trigger on a plain \"review\nthis code\" request. This skill is the entry point that finds and uses them.\n\n## When to Use\n\n- Any request to review, audit, or security-check source code\n- Any request to find vulnerabilities or check code against OWASP Top 10 / CWE\n- As the first step before the library's specialised application-security skills\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1 - Understand the code\nRead every source file in scope. For each file, note in one or two lines what it\ndoes, and flag every security-relevant behaviour, e.g.:\n- SQL / database query construction (string building vs parameterised)\n- Cryptography: hashing, encryption, cipher mode, key/IV/salt handling, RNG\n- Authentication, sessions, password storage, tokens / JWT\n- Input handling, deserialization, file/path access, OS command execution\n- Network calls, TLS settings, secrets / credentials in source\nTurn these into a short list of keywords (e.g. \"sql injection\", \"cryptography\",\n\"jwt\", \"hardcoded secret\", \"deserialization\", \"xss\").\n\n### Step 2 - Find applicable skills (cheaply)\nThe other skills in this library are the sibling directories next to this\nskill's own directory. Do NOT read them all. Instead:\n1. Find this skill's directory, then its parent (the library root).\n2. Grep skill NAMES first for your Step 1 keywords, e.g. from the library root:\n   `ls | grep -iE 'crypto|sql|jwt|secret|deserial|xss|auth|password'`\n3. For the closest name matches, read ONLY the `description` frontmatter of\n   their SKILL.md (`sed -n '1,15p' <name>/SKILL.md`).\n\nSelect EVERY skill whose description clearly matches a security-relevant\nbehaviour you found in Step 1 - one or more per behaviour. Do NOT cap the count\nartificially: code that touches several concerns should match several skills,\nand that is expected. Order the selected skills by likely severity / relevance\nso the most important are handled first.\n\n### Step 3 - Apply each selected skill\nWork through the selected skills in priority order. Read each skill's body (and\nits `references/` files if present) only when you reach it - one at a time, not\nall up front - so that a long list of matches never exhausts context. Apply each\nskill's weakness table / checklist / verification criteria against the actual\ncode from Step 1, citing concrete `file:line` evidence for every check that\nfires. Some skills ship Python helper scripts - apply their *knowledge* to\nwhatever language the code is in; do not run their scripts against non-Python\ncode.\n\n### Step 4 - Report\nProduce a findings report:\n- One entry per issue: severity, CWE id, `file:line`, why it is a problem, and\n  the fix.\n- A \"Skills applied\" list naming the specialised skills you used.\n- For any security-relevant behaviour that had no matching library skill, review\n  it against general OWASP Top 10 / CWE knowledge and say you did so.\n- If nothing is wrong, say so explicitly and list what you checked.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Report only. Do not create or modify any files unless explicitly asked.\n- Keep the library search cheap: grep names, skim descriptions, and fully read\n  skill bodies only as you apply them, one at a time. Never load the whole\n  library into context at once.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Every security-relevant behaviour from Step 1 is either matched to a skill and\n  checked, or explicitly reviewed against general knowledge.\n- Every finding carries a CWE id and a `file:line`.\n- The \"Skills applied\" list is present.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

Dedicated Cipher instance per invocation guarantees thread-safety\n   180\t            Cipher cipher = Cipher.getInstance(CIPHER_TRANSFORMATION);\n   181\t            GCMParameterSpec spec = new GCMParameterSpec(GCM_TAG_LENGTH_BITS, payload.getIv());\n   182\t            cipher.init(Cipher.DECRYPT_MODE, decryptionKey, spec);\n   183\t\n   184\t            if (aad != null && aad.length > 0) {\n   185\t                cipher.updateAAD(aad);\n   186\t            }\n   187\t\n   188\t            return cipher.doFinal(payload.getCiphertextWithTag());\n   189\t        } catch (GeneralSecurityException e) {\n   190\t            // Record failure in rate limiter to throttle brute-force attacks\n   191\t            rateLimiter.recordFailure(validatedSourceId, keyId);\n   192\t            LOGGER.log(Level.FINE, \"Decryption failed: cryptographic verification error\", e);\n   193\t            // Rule 6: Uniform generic error message prevents padding or tag oracle leakage\n   194\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Decryption failed\");\n   195\t        }\n   196\t    }\n   197\t\n   198\t    @Override\n   199\t    public byte[] decrypt(byte[] serializedPayload, byte[] aad, String sourceId) {\n   200\t        Objects.requireNonNull(serializedPayload, \"Serialized payload must not be null\");\n   201\t        EncryptedPayload payload = EncryptedPayload.fromBytes(serializedPayload, maxCiphertextBytes);\n   202\t        return decrypt(payload, aad, sourceId);\n   203\t    }\n   204\t\n   205\t    @Override\n   206\t    public byte[] payload = EncryptedPayload.fromBase64Url(base64Payload, maxCiphertextBytes);\n   209\t        return decrypt(payload, aad, sourceId);\n   210\t    }\n   211\t\n   212\t    /**\n   213\t     * Performs a dummy decryption operation using dummy key and data of comparable size.\n   214\t     * This ensures constant computational work is performed even when an invalid/unknown\n   215\t     * key ID is presented, neutralizing timing attacks that seek to enumerate valid keys.\n   216\t     */\n   217\t    private void performDummyDecryption(byte[] iv, byte[] ciphertext, byte[] aad) {\n   218\t        try {\n   219\t            Cipher cipher = Cipher.getInstance(CIPHER_TRANSFORMATION);\n   220\t            GCMParameterSpec spec = new GCMParameterSpec(GCM_TAG_LENGTH_BITS, iv);\n   221\t            cipher.init(Cipher.DECRYPT_MODE, dummyKey, spec);\n   222\t            if (aad != null && aad.length > 0) {\n   223\t                cipher.updateAAD(aad);\n   224\t            }\n   225\t            cipher.doFinal(ciphertext);\n   226\t        } catch (Exception ignored) {\n   227\t            // Expected to fail since dummyKey will not decrypt caller ciphertext;\n   228\t            // goal is 234\t     * has finished processing it (Rule 5).\n   235\t     *\n   236\t     * @param plaintext The plaintext buffer to wipe.\n   237\t     */\n   238\t    public static void wipePlaintext(byte[] plaintext) {\n   239\t        CryptoUtils.wipe(plaintext);\n   240\t    }\n   241\t}\n===== src/main/java/com/security/crypto/CryptoException.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.security.crypto;\n     2\t\n     3\t/**\n     4\t * Generic cryptographic exception thrown for all encryption, decryption,\n     5\t * key handling, and validation failures.\n     6\t * <p>\n     7\t * Security Design:\n     8\t * <ul>\n     9\t *   <li>Rule 6 (Failure behavior): To prevent oracle attacks, key-enumeration,\n    10\t *       and side-channel information leaks, caller-facing error messages are strictly\n    11\t *       uniform and generic. 27\t    public CryptoException(String safeMessage) {\n    28\t        super(safeMessage);\n    29\t    }\n    30\t\n    31\t    /**\n    32\t     * Constructs a new CryptoException with a generic safe 147\t    /**\n   148\t     * Creates a defensive copy of a byte array, or returns an empty array if input is null.\n   149\t     *\n   150\t     * @param source The source array.\n   151\t     * com.security.crypto;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentHashMap;\n     4\timport java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicInteger;\n     5\t\n     6\t/**\n     7\t * Dual-bucket, multi-dimensional failure rate limiter for decryption operations.\n     8\t * <p>\n     9\t * Security Design &amp; Attack Mitigation:\n    10\t * <ul>\n    11\t *   <li>Rule 3 (Authentication and Access): Limits repeated decryption failures per source\n    12\t *       (e.g., client IP or caller identifier) AND per account/resource (e.g., keyId or accountId).\n    13\t *       Because the source is throttled independently from the account, an attacker submitting\n    14\t *       invalid ciphertexts from an external source will be blocked at the source level\n    15\t *       before exhausting the account limit, preventing attacker-induced denial-of-service\n    16\t *       and lockout of legitimate users.</li>\n    17\t *   <li>Rule 5 (Sensitive Data): Enforces a hard bound on the internal tracker maps (bounded memory)\n    18\t *       and periodically prunes expired entries to prevent memory exhaustion DoS.</li>\n    19\t *   <li>Rule 7 (Concurrency): Employs lock-free atomic window operations via ConcurrentHashMap\n    20\t *       and AtomicInteger for race-condition-free check-and-act behavior.</li>\n    21\t * </ul>\n    22\t */\n    23\tpublic final class DecryptionRateLimiter {\n    24\t\n    25\t    /**\n    26\t     * Default window duration in milliseconds (1 minute).\n    27\t     */\n    28\t    public static final long DEFAULT_WINDOW_MS = 60_000L;\n    29\t\n    30\t    /**\n    31\t     * Default maximum permitted failures per source per window.\n    32\t     */\n    33\t    public static final int DEFAULT_MAX_SOURCE_FAILURES = 20;\n    34\t\n    35\t    /**\n    36\t     * Default maximum permitted failures per account/resource per window.\n    37\t     */\n    38\t    public static final int DEFAULT_MAX_RESOURCE_FAILURES = 50;\n    39\t\n    40\t    /**\n    41\t     * Maximum entries stored in each tracker map to prevent memory exhaustion.\n    42\t     */\n    43\t    private static final int MAX_MAP_ENTRIES = 10_000;\n    44\t\n    45\t    private final long windowDurationMs;\n    46\t    private final int maxSourceFailures;\n    47\t    private final int maxResourceFailures;\n    48\t\n    49\t    private final ConcurrentHashMap<String, WindowCounter> sourceCounters = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n    50\t    private final ConcurrentHashMap<String, WindowCounter> resourceCounters = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n    51\t\n    52\t    /**\n    53\t     * Constructs a DecryptionRateLimiter with default settings.\n    54\t     */\n    55\t    public DecryptionRateLimiter() {\n    56\t        this(DEFAULT_WINDOW_MS, DEFAULT_MAX_SOURCE_FAILURES, DEFAULT_MAX_RESOURCE_FAILURES);\n    57\t    }\n    58\t\n    59\t    /**\n    60\t     * Constructs a DecryptionRateLimiter with custom parameters.\n    61\t     *\n    62\t     * @param windowDurationMs    Window size in milliseconds.\n    63\t     * @param maxSourceFailures   Max failures allowed per source per window.\n    64\t     * @param maxResourceFailures Max failures allowed per resource/account per window.\n    65\t     */\n    66\t    public DecryptionRateLimiter(long windowDurationMs, int maxSourceFailures, int maxResourceFailures) {\n    67\t        if (windowDurationMs <= 0 || maxSourceFailures <= 0 || maxResourceFailures <= 0) {\n    68\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Rate limiter parameters must be positive integers\");\n    69\t        }\n    70\t        this.windowDurationMs = windowDurationMs;\n    71\t        this.maxSourceFailures = maxSourceFailures;\n    72\t        this.maxResourceFailures = maxResourceFailures;\n    73\t    }\n    74\t\n    75\t    /**\n    76\t     * Atomically checks whether a decryption attempt is allowed under current rate limits.\n    77\t     * Fails closed if either source or resource failure threshold is exceeded.\n    78\t     *\n    79\t     * @param sourceId   The source / caller identifier.\n    80\t     * @param resourceId The resource / key / account identifier.\n    81\t     * @throws CryptoException If rate limit is exceeded.\n    82\t     */\n    83\t    public void checkAllowed(String sourceId, String resourceId) {\n    84\t        long now = System.currentTimeMillis();\n    85\t\n    86\t        if (sourceId != null) {\n    87\t            WindowCounter sourceCounter = sourceCounters.get(sourceId);\n    88\t            if (sourceCounter != null && sourceCounter.isExceeded(now, windowDurationMs, maxSourceFailures)) {\n    89\t                throw new CryptoException(\"Decryption rate limit exceeded\");\n    90\t            }\n    91\t        }\n    92\t\n    93\t        if (resourceId != null) {\n    94\t            WindowCounter resourceCounter = resourceCounters.get(resourceId);\n    95\t            if (resourceCounter != null && resourceCounter.isExceeded(now, windowDurationMs, maxResourceFailures)) {\n    96\t                throw new CryptoException(\"Decryption rate limit exceeded\");\n    97\t            }\n    98\t        }\n    99\t    }\n   100\t\n   101\t    /**\n   102\t     * Atomically records a decryption failure against both the source and the resource.\n   103\t     *\n   104\t     * @param sourceId   The source / caller identifier.\n   105\t     * @param resourceId The resource / key / account identifier.\n   106\t     */\n   107\t    public void recordFailure(String sourceId, String resourceId) {\n   108\t        long now = System.currentTimeMillis();\n   109\t\n   110\t        if (sourceId != null) {\n   111\t            pruneIfNecessary(sourceCounters, now);\n   112\t            sourceCounters.compute(sourceId, (k, counter) -> {\n   113\t                if (counter == null || (now - counter.windowStartMs) > windowDurationMs) {\n   114\t                    return new WindowCounter(now, 1);\n   115\t                } else {\n   116\t                    counter.count.incrementAndGet();\n   117\t                    return counter;\n   118\t                }\n   119\t            });\n   120\t        }\n   121\t\n   122\t        if (resourceId != null) {\n   123\t            pruneIfNecessary(resourceCounters, now);\n   124\t            resourceCounters.compute(resourceId, (k, counter) -> {\n   125\t                if (counter == null || (now - counter.windowStartMs) > windowDurationMs) {\n   126\t                    return new WindowCounter(now, 1);\n   127\t                } else {\n   128\t                    counter.count.incrementAndGet();\n   129\t                    return counter;\n   130\t                }\n   131\t            });\n   132\t        }\n   133\t    }\n   134\t\n   135\t    /**\n   136\t     * Resets failure counters for a source and resource on success or administrative action.\n   137\t     *\n   138\t     * @param sourceId   The source identifier.\n   139\t     * @param resourceId The resource identifier.\n   140\t     */\n   141\t    public void reset(String sourceId, String resourceId) {\n   142\t        if (sourceId != null) {\n   143\t            sourceCounters.remove(sourceId);\n   144\t        }\n   145\t        if (resourceId != null) {\n   146\t            resourceCounters.remove(resourceId);\n   147\t        }\n   148\t    }\n   149\t\n   150\t    /**\n   151\t     * Bounds map memory by purging expired counters if capacity reaches threshold.\n   152\t     */\n   153\t    private void pruneIfNecessary(ConcurrentHashMap<String, WindowCounter> map, long now) {\n   154\t        if (map.size() >= MAX_MAP_ENTRIES) {\n   155\t            map.entrySet().removeIf(entry -> (now - entry.getValue().windowStartMs) > windowDurationMs);\n   156\t        }\n   157\t    }\n   158\t\n   159\t    /**\n   160\t     * Internal window counter.\n   161\t     */\n   162\t    private static final class WindowCounter {\n   163\t        final long windowStartMs;\n   164\t        final AtomicInteger count;\n   165\t\n   166\t        WindowCounter(long windowStartMs, int initialCount) {\n   167\t            this.windowStartMs = windowStartMs;\n   168\t            this.count = new AtomicInteger(initialCount);\n   169\t        }\n   170\t\n   171\t        boolean isExceeded(long now, long windowMs, int maxLimit) {\n   172\t            if ((now - windowStartMs) > windowMs) {\n   173\t                return false; // Window expired\n   174\t            }\n   175\t            return count.get() >= maxLimit;\n   176\t        }\n   177\t    }\n   178\t}\n===== src/main/java/com/security/crypto/EncryptedPayload.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.security.crypto;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.nio.ByteBuffer;\n     4\timport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\n     5\timport java.util.Arrays;\n     6\timport java.util.Base64;\n     7\timport java.util.Objects;\n     8\t\n     9\t/**\n    10\t * Encapsulates authenticated ciphertext, initialization vector (IV), version,\n    11\t * and key metadata in a tamper-evident binary or URL-safe Base64 envelope.\n    12\t * <p>\n    13\t * Wire Format (Version 1):\n    14\t * <pre>\n    15\t * +---------------+--------------+----------------+---------------+---------------+----------+--------------------+---------------------+\n    16\t * | Magic (4B)    | Version (1B) | KeyIdLen (1B)  | KeyId (NB)    | IvLen (1B)    | IV (12B) | CipherLen (4B)     | Ciphertext+Tag (MB) |\n    17\t * | 'S','E','C','1'| 0x01         | 1..64          | UTF-8 bytes   | 0x0C (12)     | 12 bytes | Big-endian integer | >= 16 bytes         |\n    18\t * +---------------+--------------+----------------+---------------+---------------+----------+--------------------+---------------------+\n    19\t * </pre>\n    20\t * <p>\n    21\t * Security Design:\n    22\t * <ul>\n    23\t *   <li>Rule 1 (Trust Boundaries): Strictly validates magic bytes, version, lengths,\n    24\t *       and character boundaries. preventing leakage in logs, monitoring, or debug traces.</li>\n    27\t *   <li>Rule 6 (Failure Behavior): Malformed payloads trigger uniform {@link CryptoException}\n    28\t *       rejections with    */\n    36\t    public static final byte[] MAGIC = new byte[]{'S', 'E', 'C', '1'};\n    37\t\n    38\t    /**\n    39\t     * Payload wire format version.\n    40\t     */\n    41\t    public   52\t\n    53\t    /**\n    54\t     * Minimum valid binary wire size (Magic + Version + KeyIdLen + MinKeyId + IvLen + IV + CipherLen + MinCiphertextTag).\n    55\t     */\n    56\t    private static final int MIN_WIRE_LENGTH = 4 + 1 + 1 + 1 + 1 + GCM_IV_LENGTH + 4 + GCM_TAG_LENGTH;\n    57\t\n    58\t    private final byte version;\n    59\t    private final String keyId;\n    60\t    private final byte[] iv;\n    61\t    private final byte[] ciphertextWithTag;\n    62\t\n    63\t    /**\n    64\t     * Constructs an EncryptedPayload.\n    65\t     *\n    66\t     * @param version           The wire protocol version.\n    67\t     * @param keyId             The key identifier used to encrypt the payload.\n    68\t     * @param iv                The 12-byte initialization vector.\n    69\t     * @param ciphertextWithTag The ciphertext including the 16-byte GCM authentication tag.\n    70\t     */\n    71\t    public EncryptedPayload(byte version, String keyId, byte[] iv, byte[] ciphertextWithTag) {\n    72\t        if (version != CURRENT_VERSION) {\n    73\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Invalid cryptographic request: unsupported payload version\");\n    74\t        }\n    75\t        this.version = version;\n    76\t        this.keyId = CryptoUtils.validateKeyId(keyId);\n    77\t\n    78\t        Objects.requireNonNull(iv, \"IV must not be null\");\n    79\t {\n    86\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Invalid cryptographic request: ciphertext shorter than authentication tag\");\n    87\t        }\n    88\t        this.ciphertextWithTag = CryptoUtils.copyDefensive(ciphertextWithTag);\n    89\t    }\n    90\t\n    91\t    public byte getVersion() {\n    92\t        return version;\n    93\t    }\n    94\t\n    95\t    public String getKeyId() {\n    96\t        return keyId;\n    97\t    }\n    98\t\n    99\t    public byte[] getIv() {\n   100\t        return CryptoUtils.copyDefensive(iv);\n   101\t    }\n   102\t\n   103\t standard binary wire format.\n   109\t     *\n   110\t     * @return Serialized byte array.\n   111\t     */\n   112\t    public byte[] toBytes() {\n   113\t        byte[] keyIdBytes = keyId.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n   114\t        int totalLength = 4 + 1 + 1 + keyIdBytes.length + 1 + iv.length + 4 + ciphertextWithTag.length;\n   115\t        ByteBuffer buffer = ByteBuffer.allocate(totalLength);\n   116\t        buffer.put(MAGIC);\n   117\t        buffer.put(version);\n   118\t        buffer.put((byte) keyIdBytes.length);\n   119\t        buffer.put(keyIdBytes);\n   120\t        buffer.put((byte) iv.length);\n   121\t        buffer.put(iv);\n   122\t        buffer.putInt(ciphertextWithTag.length);\n   123\t        buffer.put(ciphertextWithTag);\n   124\t        return buffer.array();\n   125\t    }\n   126\t\n   127\t    /**\n   128\t     * Serializes this payload to a URL-safe Base64 string without padding.\n Base64.getUrlEncoder().withoutPadding().encodeToString(toBytes());\n   134\t    }\n   135\t\n   136\t    /**\n   137\t     * Parses and strictly validates an EncryptedPayload from binary wire format.\n   138\t     *\n   139\t     * @param data              Raw byte array from untrusted input.\n   140\t     * @param maxCiphertextSize Maximum permissible ciphertext size to prevent OOM DoS attacks.\n   141\t     * @return Validated EncryptedPayload instance.\n   142\t     * @throws CryptoException If the input fails validation or integrity checks.\n   143\t     */\n   144\t    public static EncryptedPayload fromBytes(byte[] data, int maxCiphertextSize) {\n   145\t        if (data == null || data.length < MIN_WIRE_LENGTH) {\n   146\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Invalid cryptographic payload: payload too short or malformed\");\n   147\t        }\n   148\t        if (data.length > maxCiphertextSize + 1024) {\n   149\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Invalid cryptographic payload: payload exceeds maximum allowable size\");\n   150\t        }\n   151\t\n   152\t        try {\n   153\t            ByteBuffer buffer = ByteBuffer.wrap(data);\n   154\t\n   155\t            // 1. Verify Magic Header\n   156\t            byte[] magicHeader = new byte[4];\n   157\t            buffer.get(magicHeader);\n   158\t            if (!Arrays.equals(magicHeader, MAGIC)) {\n   159\t                throw new CryptoException(\"Invalid cryptographic payload: unrecognized format header\");\n   160\t            }\n   161\t\n   162\t            // 2. Verify KeyId\n   169\t            int keyIdLen = buffer.get() & 0xFF;\n   170\t            if (keyIdLen < 1 || keyIdLen > 64 || buffer.remaining() < keyIdLen) {\n   171\t                throw new CryptoException(\"Invalid cryptographic payload: invalid key identifier length\");\n   172\t            }\n   173\t            byte[] keyIdBytes = new byte[keyIdLen];\n   174\t            buffer.get(keyIdBytes);\n   175\t            String parsedKeyId = new String(keyIdBytes, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n   176\t            CryptoUtils.validateKeyId(parsedKeyId);\n   177\t\n   178\t            // 4. Verify IV\n   179\t            if (buffer.remaining() < 1) {\n   180\t                throw new CryptoException(\"Invalid cryptographic payload: truncated payload header\");\n   181\t            }\n   182\t            int ivLen = buffer.get() & 0xFF;\n   183\t            if (ivLen != GCM_IV_LENGTH || buffer.remaining() < ivLen) {\n   184\t                throw new CryptoException(\"Invalid cryptographic payload: invalid IV length\");\n   185\t            }\n   186\t            byte[] parsedIv = new byte[ivLen];\n   187\t            buffer.get(parsedIv);\n   188\t\n   189\t            // 5. Verify Ciphertext & Tag\n   190\t            if (buffer.remaining() < 4) {\n   191\t                throw new CryptoException(\"Invalid cryptographic payload: truncated payload header\");\n   192\t CryptoException(\"Invalid cryptographic payload: invalid ciphertext length\");\n   196\t            }\n   197\t            if (buffer.remaining() != cipherLen) {\n   198\t                // Must match exactly: reject extra trailing bytes or truncation\n   199\t                throw new CryptoException(\"Invalid cryptographic payload: payload length mismatch\");\n   200\t            }\n   201\t            byte[] parsedCiphertext = new byte[cipherLen];\n   202\t            buffer.get(parsedCiphertext);\n   203\t\n   204\t            return new EncryptedPayload(ver, parsedKeyId, parsedIv, parsedCiphertext);\n   205\t        } catch (java.nio.BufferUnderflowException e) {\n   206\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Invalid cryptographic payload: truncated payload\", e);\n   207\t        }\n   208\t    }\n   209\t\n   210\t    /**\n   211\t     * Parses and strictly validates an EncryptedPayload from a URL-safe Base64 string.\n   212\t     *\n   213\t     * @param base64Url         Untrusted Base64 string.\n   214\t     * @param maxCiphertextSize Maximum permissible ciphertext size.\n   215\t     * @return Validated EncryptedPayload instance.\n   216\t     * @throws CryptoException If Base64 decoding or structure validation fails.\n   217\t     */\n   218\t    public static EncryptedPayload fromBase64Url(String base64Url, int maxCiphertextSize) {\n   219\t on base64 string length to avoid allocating oversized arrays\n   223\t        int maxBase64Len = ((maxCiphertextSize + 1024) * 4) / 3 + 4;\n   224\t        if (base64Url.length() > maxBase64Len) {\n   225\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Invalid cryptographic payload: input exceeds maximum allowable size\");\n   226\t        }\n   227\t\n   228\t        byte[] raw;\n   229\t        try {\n   230\t            raw = Base64.getUrlDecoder().decode(base64Url);\n   231\t        } catch (IllegalArgumentException e) {\n   232\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Invalid cryptographic payload: invalid Base64 encoding\", e);\n   233\t        }\n   234\t\n   235\t        return fromBytes(raw, maxCiphertextSize);\n   236\t    }\n   237\t\n   238\t    /**\n   239\t     * Masks sensitive payload contents to prevent accidental disclosure in logs,\n   240\t     * exceptions, or debug inspection (Rules 2 &amp; 5).\n   241\t     */\n   242\t    @Override\n   243\t    public String toString() {\n   244\t        return \"EncryptedPayload[version=\" + version\n   245\t                + \", keyId='\" + keyId + '\\''\n   246\t                + \", ivLength=\" + iv.length\n   247\t                + \", ciphertextLength=\" + ciphertextWithTag.length\n   248\t                + \", content=[PROTECTED]]\";\n   249\t    }\n   250\t}\n===== src/main/java/com/security/crypto/FileSecurityUtil.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.security.crypto;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.io.File;\n     4\timport java.io.IOException;\n     5\timport owner-private file permissions.\n    18\t * <p>\n    19\t * Satisfies:\n    20\t * <ul>\n    21\t *   <li>Rule 1 (Trust Boundaries): Validates file paths, canonicalization, and regular file status.</li>\n    22\t *   <li>Rule 2 (Secrets): Rejects files readable by group or other users.</li>\n    23\t *   <li>Rule 5 (Sensitive Data): Ensures files containing sensitive data are created 27\tpublic final class FileSecurityUtil {\n    28\t\n    29\t    private static final boolean IS_POSIX = FileSystems.getDefault().supportedFileAttributeViews().contains(\"posix\");\n    30\t\n    31\t    private FileSecurityUtil() {\n    32\t        // Utility class\n    33\t    }\n    34\t\n    35\t    /**\n    36\t     * Validates that a file containing cryptographic keys or secrets exists, is a regular file,\n    37\t     * is not located in an insecure/world-writable location, and is strictly private to the owner.\n    38\t     *\n    39\t     * @param path Path to validate.\n    40\t     * @return Canonical normalized Path.\n    41\t     * @throws CryptoException If the path fails security or permission validation.\n    42\t     */\n    43\t    public static Path validatePrivateFile(Path path) {\n    44\t        if (path == null) {\n    45\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Invalid cryptographic request: file path must not be null\");\n    46\t        }\n    47\t\n    48\t        Path normalized;\n    49\t        try {\n    50\t            normalized = path.toAbsolutePath().normalize();\n    51\t            if (!Files.exists(normalized, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n    52\t                throw new CryptoException(\"Key file does not exist\");\n    53\t            }\n    54\t            if (!Files.isRegularFile(normalized, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n    55\t                throw new CryptoException(\"Key file is not a regular file\");\n    56\t            }\n    57\t        } catch (SecurityException e) {\n    58\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Security manager denied access to key file\", e);\n    59\t        }\n    60\t\n    61\t        // Validate POSIX file permissions and parent directory safety if supported\n    62\t        if (IS_POSIX) {\n    63\t            try {\n    64\t                // Check parent directory is not world-writable\n    65\t                Path parent = normalized.getParent();\n    66\t                if (parent != null && Files.exists(parent)) {\n    67\t                    Set<PosixFilePermission> parentPerms = Files.getPosixFilePermissions(parent);\n    68\t                    if (parentPerms.contains(PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_WRITE)) {\n    69\t                        throw new CryptoException(\"Key file null && !currentUser.equals(owner.getName()) && !\"root\".equals(owner.getName())) {\n    77\t                    throw new CryptoException(\"Key file is not owned by the current application user\");\n    78\t                }\n    79\t\n    80\t                // Check file permissions: must not be readable/writable by group or others\n    81\t                Set<PosixFilePermission> perms = Files.getPosixFilePermissions(normalized, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS);\n    82\t                if (perms.contains(PosixFilePermission.GROUP_READ)\n    83\t                        || perms.contains(PosixFilePermission.GROUP_WRITE)\n    84\t                        || perms.contains(PosixFilePermission.GROUP_EXECUTE)\n    85\t                        || perms.contains(PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_READ)\n    86\t                        || perms.contains(PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_WRITE)\n    87\t                        || perms.contains(PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_EXECUTE)) {\n    88\t                    throw new CryptoException(\"Key file permissions are too open: must be readable only by the owner (mode 0600 or 0400)\");\n    89\t                }\n    90\t            } catch (IOException e) {\n    91\t                throw new CryptoException(\"Failed to verify key file security attributes\", e);\n    92\t            }\n    93\t        }\n    94\t\n    95\t        return normalized;\n    96\t    }\n    97\t\n    98\t    /**\n    99\t     * Atomically creates a file strictly private to the owner (mode file.setReadable(false, false)\n   119\t                    && file.setReadable(true, true)\n   120\t                    && file.setWritable(false, false)\n   121\t                    && file.setWritable(true, true)\n   122\t                    && file.setExecutable(false, false);\n   123\t            if (!ok) {\n   124\t                // If setting permissions failed, fail closed\n ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n    28\t    private final AtomicReference<String> activeKeyId = new AtomicReference<>(null);\n    29\t    private final AtomicBoolean closed = new AtomicBoolean(false);\n    30\t\n    31\t    /**\n    32\t     * Constructs an empty InMemoryKeyProvider.\n    33\t     */\n    34\t    public InMemoryKeyProvider() {\n    35\t    }\n    36\t\n    37\t    /**\n    38\t     * null\");\n    56\t        if (!REQUIRED_ALGORITHM.equalsIgnoreCase(key.getAlgorithm())) {\n    57\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Invalid cryptographic key: algorithm must be \" + REQUIRED_ALGORITHM);\n    58\t        }\n    59\t        byte[] encoded = key.getEncoded();\n    60\t        if (encoded != null) {\n    61\t            try {\n    62\t                if    @Override\n   152\t    public Optional<SecretKey> getKey(String keyId) {\n   153\t        ensureOpen();\n   154\t        if (keyId == null) {\n   155\t            return Optional.empty();\n   156\t ensureOpen();\n   163\t        if (keyId == null) {\n   164\t            return false;\n   165\t        }\n   166\t        return keys.containsKey(keyId);\n   167\t    }\n   168\t\n   169\t 12\t/**\n    13\t * Key derivation function using platform standard PBKDF2WithHmacSHA512.\n    14\t * <p>\n    15\t * Satisfies:\n    16\t * <ul>\n    17\t *   <li>Rule 1 (Trust Boundaries): Enforces Number of salt bytes (must be &gt;= 16).\n    59\t     * @return Generated salt byte array.\n    60\t     */\n    61\t    public static byte[] generateSalt(int length) {\n    62\t        if (length <    66\t        SECURE_RANDOM.nextBytes(salt);\n    67\t        return salt;\n    68\t    }\n    69\t\n    70\t    /**\n    71\t     * Generates a default 16-byte cryptographically secure salt.\n    72\t     *\n    73\t     * @return 16-byte random salt.\n    74\t     */\n    75\t    public static byte[] generateSalt() {\n    76\t        return generateSalt(MIN_SALT_BYTES);\n    77\t    }\n    78\t\n    79\t    /**\n    80\t     * Derives an AES-256 SecretKey from a passphrase and salt using default iteration count.\n    81\t     *\n    82\t     * @param passphrase Passphrase characters (will be zeroed upon completion).\n    83\t     * @param salt       Cryptographic salt (must be &gt;= 16 bytes).\n    84\t     * @return 256-bit AES SecretKey.\n    85\t     */\n    86\t    public static SecretKey deriveKey(char[] passphrase, byte[] salt) {\n    87\t        return deriveKey(passphrase, salt, DEFAULT_ITERATIONS);\n    88\t    }\n    89\t\n    90\t    /**\n    91\t     * Derives an AES-256 SecretKey from a passphrase, salt, and specified iteration count.\n    92\t     *\n    93\t     * @param passphrase Passphrase characters (will be zeroed upon completion).\n    94\t     * @param salt       Cryptographic salt (must be &gt;= 16 bytes).\n    95\t     * @param iterations Iteration count (must be &gt;= 210,000).\n    96\t     * @return 256-bit AES SecretKey.\n    97\t     */\n    98\t    public static SecretKey deriveKey(char[] passphrase, byte[] salt, int iterations) {\n    99\t        Objects.requireNonNull(passphrase, \"Passphrase must not be null\");\n   100\t        Objects.requireNonNull(salt, \"Salt must not be null\");\n   101\t\n   102\t        if (salt.length < MIN_SALT_BYTES) {\n   103\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Salt must be at least \" + MIN_SALT_BYTES + \" bytes\");\n   104\t        }\n   105\t        if (iterations < MIN_ITERATIONS) {\n   106\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Iteration count must be at least \" + MIN_ITERATIONS);\n   107\t        }\n   108\t\n   109\t        PBEKeySpec spec = null;\n   110\t        byte[] encodedKey = null;\n   111\t        try {\n   112\t            spec = new PBEKeySpec(passphrase, salt, iterations, DERIVED_KEY_BITS);\n   113\t            SecretKeyFactory factory = SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(KDF_ALGORITHM);\n   114\t            SecretKey pbeKey = factory.generateSecret(spec);\n   115\t            encodedKey = pbeKey.getEncoded();\n   116\t            return new SecretKeySpec(encodedKey, \"AES\");\n   117\t        } catch (NoSuchAlgorithmException | InvalidKeySpecException e) {\n   118\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Key derivation failed\", e);\n   119\t        } finally {\n   120\t            if (spec != null) {\n   121\t                spec.clearPassword();\n   122\t            }\n   123\t            CryptoUtils.wipe(passphrase);\n   124\t            CryptoUtils.wipe(encodedKey);\n   125\t        }\n   126\t    }\n   127\t}\n===== src/main/java/com/security/crypto/KeyGeneratorUtil.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.security.crypto;\n     2\t\n     3\timport javax.crypto.KeyGenerator;\n     4\timport javax.crypto.SecretKey;\n     5\timport java.security.NoSuchAlgorithmException;\n     6\timport java.security.SecureRandom;\n     7\timport java.util.HexFormat;\n     8\t\n     9\t/**\n    10\t * algorithm unavailable\", e);\n    40\t        }\n    41\t    }\n    42\t\n    43\t    /**\n    44\t     * Generates a cryptographically strong, random key identifier.\n    45\t     * Prefixed with   SECURE_RANDOM.nextBytes(iv);\n    63\t        return iv;\n    64\t    }\n    65\t}\n===== src/main/java/com/security/crypto/KeyProvider.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.security.crypto;\n     2\t\n     3\timport javax.crypto.SecretKey;\n     4\timport java.util.Optional;\n     5\t\n     6\t/**\n     7\t * Interface representing a secure provider for symmetric keys used in encryption and decryption.\n     8\t * <p>\n     9\t * Key Lifecycle &amp; Rotation (Rules 1, 2, 3, 5):\n    10\t * <ul>\n    11\t *   <li>Supports key rotation: a single \"active\" key is used for new encryptions,\n    12\t *       while historical keys remain accessible for decrypting existing records.</li>\n    13\t *   <li>Keys are resolved strictly against server-side state. Unknown or revoked\n    14\t *       keys result in immediate failure closed.</li>\n    15\t *   <li>Implements {@link AutoCloseable} to guarantee zeroization and destruction\n    16\t *       of key material from memory when decommissioned.</li>\n    17\t * </ul>\n    18\t */\n    19\tpublic interface KeyProvider extends AutoCloseable {\n    20\t\n    21\t    /**\n    22\t     * Retrieves the available.\n    26\t     */\n    27\t    SecretKey getActiveKey();\n    28\t\n    29\t    /**\n    30\t     * Retrieves the key identifier for the currently active key.\n    31\t     *\n    32\t     *   * Retrieves a key by its identifier for decryption.\n    39\t     *\n    40\t     * @param keyId The identifier of the key to look up.\n    41\t     * @return An Optional containing the key if found, or empty if unknown/revoked.\n    42\t     */\n    43\t    Optional<SecretKey> getKey(String keyId);\n    44\t\n    45\t    /**\n    46\t     * Checks if a key exists and is available in this hasKey(String keyId);\n    52\t\n    53\t    /**\n    54\t     * Closes the key provider and clears/destroys all underlying key material in memory.\n    55\t     */\n    56\t    @Override\n    57\t    void close();\n    58\t}\n===== src/main/java/com/security/crypto/KeyStoreKeyProvider.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.security.crypto;\n     2\t\n     3\timport javax.crypto.SecretKey;\n     4\timport KeyStoreKeyProvider fromInputStream(InputStream inputStream, char[] password, String activeKeyAlias) {\n    67\t        Objects.requireNonNull(inputStream, \"InputStream must not be null\");\n    68\t        Objects.requireNonNull(password, \"Password must not be null\");\n    69\t        Objects.requireNonNull(activeKeyAlias, \"Active key alias must not be null\");\n    70\t\n    71\t }\n    96\t                    }\n    97\t                }\n    98\t            }\n    99\t\n   100\t            // Verify active key alias exists\n   101\t            CryptoUtils.validateKeyId(activeKeyAlias);\n   102\t            if (!provider.keys.containsKey(activeKeyAlias)) {\n   103\t                provider.close();\n   104\t                throw new CryptoUtils.wipe(password);\n   117\t        }\n   118\t    }\n   119\t\n   120\t    private void ensureOpen() {\n   121\t        if (closed.get()) {\n   122\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Key provider has been closed and destroyed\");\n   123\t        }\n   124\t    }\n   125\t\n   126\t    @Override\n   127\t    public SecretKey getActiveKey() {\n   128\t   184\t    }\n   185\t}\n===== src/main/java/com/security/crypto/SymmetricEncryptionService.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.security.crypto;\n     2\t\n     3\t/**\n     4\t * High-level service @Test\n   331\t    @DisplayName(\"Oversized payload rejected to prevent DoS/OOM\")\n   332\t    public void testOversizedPayloadRejected() {\n   333\t        int smallMax = 100;\n   334\t 343\t    // Brute-Force Rate Limiting & User Lockout Prevention Tests (Rule 3 & Rule 7)\n   344\t    // =========================================================================\n   345\t\n   346\t    @Test\n   347\t    @DisplayName(\"Decryption rate limiter blocks attacker source without locking out legitimate source\")\n   348\t    public void testRateLimitingPreventsLockoutOfLegitimateUser() {\n   349\t        // Configure tight rate limiter: max 3 failures per source, max 10 failures per resource\n   350\t        DecryptionRateLimiter tightLimiter = new DecryptionRateLimiter(60_000L, 3, 10);\n   351\t        AesGcmEncryptionService rateLimitedService = new AesGcmEncryptionService(\n   352\t                keyProvider, tightLimiter, 1024 * 1024, 1024 * 1024, 1024);\n   353\t\n   354\t        byte[] validPlaintext = \"Confidential data\".getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n   355\t        EncryptedPayload validPayload = rateLimitedService.encrypt(validPlaintext);\n   356\t\n   357\t        // Prepare invalid payload targeting the same key\n   358\t        byte[] corruptedCiphertext = validPayload.getCiphertextWithTag();\n   359\t        corruptedCiphertext[0] ^= 0x01;\n   360\t        EncryptedPayload corruptedPayload = new EncryptedPayload(\n   361\t                validPayload.getVersion(), validPayload.getKeyId(), validPayload.getIv(), corruptedCiphertext);\n   362\t\n   363\t        String attackerSource = \"192.168.1.100\";\n   364\t        String legitimateSource = \"192.168.1.200\";\n   365\t\n   366\t        // Attacker triggers 3 consecutive failures\n   367\t        for (int i = 0; i < 3; i++) {\n   368\t            assertThrows(CryptoException.class,\n   369\t                    () -> rateLimitedService.decrypt(corruptedPayload, null, attackerSource));\n   370\t        }\n   371\t\n   372\t        // Attacker's 4th attempt is blocked by source rate limit\n   373\t        CryptoException ex = assertThrows(CryptoException.class,\n   374\t                () -> rateLimitedService.decrypt(corruptedPayload, null, attackerSource));\n   375\t        assertTrue(ex.getMessage().contains(\"rate limit exceeded\"));\n   376\t\n   377\t        // Crucial Rule 3 Test: Legitimate source decrypting valid payload MUST SUCCEED!\n   378\t        // Attacker's failure flood did NOT lock out legitimate user on the same key/resource.\n   379\t        byte[] legitDecrypted = rateLimitedService.decrypt(validPayload, null, legitimateSource);\n   380\t        assertArrayEquals(validPlaintext, legitDecrypted);\n   381\t        CryptoUtils.wipe(legitDecrypted);\n   382\t    }\n   383\t\n   384\t    // =========================================================================\n   385\t    // Key Derivation Tests (Rule 1, 2, 5, 8)\n   386\t    // =========================================================================\n   387\t\n   388\t    @Test\n   389\t    @DisplayName(\"Key derivation produces valid 256-bit AES key and zeroes password\")\n   390\t    public void testKeyDerivation() {\n   391\t        char[] password = \"StrongApplicationSecretPassphrase#2026!\".toCharArray();\n   392\t        byte[] salt = KeyDerivation.generateSalt(16);\n   393\t\n   394\t        SecretKey derivedKey = KeyDerivation.deriveKey(password, salt, 210_000);\n   395\t        assertNotNull(derivedKey);\n   396\t        assertEquals(\"AES\", derivedKey.getAlgorithm());\n   397\t        assertEquals(32, derivedKey.getEncoded().length);\n   398\t\n   399\t        // Passphrase char array must be wiped to '\\0'\n   400\t        for (char c : password) {\n   401\t            assertEquals('\\0', c, \"Password memory must be cleared\");\n   402\t        }\n   403\t    }\n   404\t\n   405\t    @Test\n   406\t    @DisplayName(\"Key derivation rejects weak parameters (short salt or low iterations)\")\n   407\t    public void testKeyDerivationValidation() {\n   408\t        char[] password = \"ValidPassword123\".toCharArray();\n   409\t        byte[] shortSalt = new byte[8];\n   410\t\n   411\t        assertThrows(CryptoException.class, () -> KeyDerivation.deriveKey(password, shortSalt, 210_000));\n   412\t        assertThrows(CryptoException.class, () -> KeyDerivation.deriveKey(password, KeyDerivation.generateSalt(), 50_000));\n   413\t    }\n   414\t\n   415\t    // =========================================================================\n   416\t    // KeyStore Integration Tests (Rule 1, 2, 5, 8)\n   417\t    // =========================================================================\n   418\t\n   419\t    @Test\n   420\t    @DisplayName(\"KeyStoreKeyProvider loads PKCS12 keystore from stream and decrypts\")\n   421\t    public void testKeyStoreProviderFromStream() throws Exception {\n   422\t        // Programmatically generate a test PKCS12 keystore\n   423\t        KeyStore ks = KeyStore.getInstance(\"PKCS12\");\n   424\t        ks.load(null, null);\n   425\t\n   426\t        String alias = \"my_app_key\";\n   427\t        SecretKey appKey = +---------------+--------------+----------------+---------------+---------------+----------+--------------------+---------------------+\n    16\t * | Magic (4B)    | Version (1B) | KeyIdLen (1B)  |        }\n   157\t        return Optional.ofNullable(keys.get(keyId));\n   158\t    }\n   159\t\n   160\t    @Override\n   161\t    public boolean hasKey(String keyId) {\n   162\t 4\timport javax.crypto.SecretKeyFactory;\n     5\timport javax.crypto.spec.PBEKeySpec;\n     6\timport javax.crypto.spec.SecretKeySpec;\n     7\timport java.security.NoSuchAlgorithmException;\n     8\timport java.security.SecureRandom;\n     9\timport java.security.spec.InvalidKeySpecException;\n    10\timport java.util.Objects;\n    11\t\n    12\t/**\n    13\t * Key derivation function using platform standard PBKDF2WithHmacSHA512.\n    14\t * <p>\n    15\t * Satisfies:\n    16\t * <ul>\n    17\t *   <li>Rule 1 (Trust Boundaries): Enforces strict minimum bounds on iteration count and salt length.</li>\n    18\t *   <li>Rule 2 & 5 (Secrets & Sensitive Data): Zeroes passphrase char arrays and derived byte buffers from memory.</li>\n    19\t *   <li>Rule 8 (Prefer the Platform): Utilizes standard JCA PBKDF2WithHmacSHA512.</li>\n    20\t * </ul>\n    21\t */\n    22\tpublic final class KeyDerivation {\n    23\t\n    24\t    /**\n    25\t     * Standard platform PBKDF2 algorithm using PBKDF2.\n    31\t     */\n    32\t    public static final int DEFAULT_ITERATIONS = 600_000;\n    33\t\n    34\t    /**\n    35\t     * Hard lower bound for iterations to prevent weak key derivation.\n    36\t     */\n    37\t    public static final int MIN_ITERATIONS = 210_000;\n    38\t\n    39\t    /**\n    40\t     * Required salt size in bytes (128 bits).\n    41\t     */\n    42\t    public static final int MIN_SALT_BYTES = 16;\n    43\t\n    44\t    /**\n    45\t     * Derived key length in bits for AES-256.\n    46\t     */\n    47\t    public static final int DERIVED_KEY_BITS = 256;\n    48\t\n    49\t    private static final SecureRandom SECURE_RANDOM = new SecureRandom();\n    50\t\n    51\t    private KeyDerivation() {\n    52\t        // Utility class\n    53\t    }\n    54\t\n    55\t    /**\n    56\t     * Generates a cryptographically secure random salt of the specified length.\n    57\t     *\n    58\t     * @param length Number of salt bytes (must be &gt;= 16).\n    59\t     * @return Generated salt byte array.\n    60\t     */\n    61\t    public static byte[] generateSalt(int length) {\n    62\t        if (length < MIN_SALT_BYTES) {\n    63\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Salt length must be at least \" + MIN_SALT_BYTES + \" bytes\");\n    64\t        }\n    65\t        byte[] salt = new byte[length];\n    66\t        SECURE_RANDOM.nextBytes(salt);\n    67\t        return salt;\n    68\t    }\n    69\t\n    70\t    /**\n    71\t     * Generates a default 16-byte cryptographically secure salt.\n    72\t     *\n    73\t     * @return 16-byte random salt.\n    74\t     */\n    75\t    public static byte[] generateSalt() {\n    76\t        return generateSalt(MIN_SALT_BYTES);\n    77\t    }\n    78\t\n    79\t    /**\n    80\t     * Derives an AES-256 SecretKey from a passphrase and salt using default iteration count.\n    81\t     *\n    82\t     * @param passphrase Passphrase characters (will be zeroed upon completion).\n    83\t     * @param salt       Cryptographic salt (must be &gt;= 16 bytes).\n    84\t     * @return 256-bit AES SecretKey.\n    85\t     */\n    86\t    public static SecretKey deriveKey(char[] passphrase, byte[] salt) {\n    87\t        return deriveKey(passphrase, salt, DEFAULT_ITERATIONS);\n    88\t    }\n    89\t\n    90\t    /**\n    91\t     * Derives an AES-256 SecretKey from a passphrase, salt, and specified iteration count.\n    92\t     *\n    93\t     * @param passphrase Passphrase characters (will be zeroed upon completion).\n    94\t     * @param salt       Cryptographic salt (must be &gt;= 16 bytes).\n    95\t     * @param iterations Iteration count (must be &gt;= 210,000).\n    96\t     * @return 256-bit AES SecretKey.\n    97\t     */\n    98\t    public static SecretKey deriveKey(char[] passphrase, byte[] salt, int iterations) {\n    99\t        Objects.requireNonNull(passphrase, \"Passphrase must not be null\");\n   100\t        Objects.requireNonNull(salt, \"Salt must not be null\");\n   101\t\n   102\t        if (salt.length < MIN_SALT_BYTES) {\n   103\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Salt must be at least \" + MIN_SALT_BYTES + \" bytes\");\n   104\t        }\n   105\t        if (iterations < MIN_ITERATIONS) {\n   106\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Iteration count must be at least \" + MIN_ITERATIONS);\n   107\t        }\n   108\t\n   109\t        PBEKeySpec spec = null;\n   110\t        byte[] encodedKey = null;\n   111\t        try {\n   112\t            spec = new PBEKeySpec(passphrase, salt, iterations, DERIVED_KEY_BITS);\n   113\t            SecretKeyFactory factory = SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(KDF_ALGORITHM);\n   114\t            SecretKey pbeKey = factory.generateSecret(spec);\n   115\t            encodedKey = pbeKey.getEncoded();\n   116\t            return new SecretKeySpec(encodedKey, \"AES\");\n   117\t        } catch (NoSuchAlgorithmException | InvalidKeySpecException e) {\n   118\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Key derivation failed\", e);\n   119\t        } finally {\n   120\t            if (spec != null) {\n   121\t                spec.clearPassword();\n   122\t            }\n   123\t            CryptoUtils.wipe(passphrase);\n   124\t            CryptoUtils.wipe(encodedKey);\n   125\t        }\n   126\t    }\n   127\t}\n===== KeyGeneratorUtil.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.security.crypto;\n     2\t\n     3\timport javax.crypto.KeyGenerator;\n     4\timport javax.crypto.SecretKey;\n     5\timport java.security.NoSuchAlgorithmException;\n     6\timport java.security.SecureRandom;\n     7\timport java.util.HexFormat;\n     8\t\n     9\t/**\n    10\t * Utility for generating 18\tpublic final class KeyGeneratorUtil {\n    19\t\n    20\t    private static final String ALGORITHM = \"AES\";\n    21\t    private static final int KEY_SIZE_BITS = 256;\n    22\t    private static          return keyGen.generateKey();\n    38\t        } catch (NoSuchAlgorithmException e) {\n    39\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Failed to generate AES key: algorithm unavailable\", e);\n    40\t        }\n    41\t    }\n    42\t\n    43\t    /**\n    44\t     * Generates a cryptographically strong, random key identifier.\n    45\t     * Prefixed with \"k_\" followed by 16 random javax.crypto.SecretKey;\n     4\timport java.util.Optional;\n     5\t\n     6\t/**\n     7\t * Interface representing a secure provider for symmetric keys used in encryption and decryption.\n     8\t * <p>\n     9\t * Key Lifecycle &amp; Rotation (Rules 1, 2, 3, 5):\n    10\t * <ul>\n    11\t *   <li>Supports key rotation: a single \"active\" key is used for new encryptions,\n    12\t *       while historical keys remain accessible for decrypting existing records.</li>\n    13\t *   <li>Keys are resolved strictly against server-side state. Unknown or revoked\n    14\t *       keys result in immediate failure closed.</li>\n    15\t *   <li>Implements {@link AutoCloseable} to guarantee zeroization and destruction\n    16\t *       of key available.\n    26\t     */\n    27\t    SecretKey getActiveKey();\n    28\t\n    29\t    /**\n    30\t     * Retrieves the key identifier for the currently active key.\n    31\t     *\n    32\t     * found, or empty if unknown/revoked.\n    42\t     */\n    43\t    Optional<SecretKey> getKey(String keyId);\n    44\t\n    45\t    /**\n    46\t     * Checks if a key exists and is available in this provider.\n    47\t     *\n    48\t     * @param keyId The identifier of the key.\n    49\t     * @return true if the key is present and valid; false otherwise.\n    50\t     */\n    51\t    boolean hasKey(String keyId);\n    52\t\n    53\t    /**\n    54\t     * Closes the key provider and clears/destroys all underlying key material in memory.\n    55\t     */\n    56\t    @Override\n    57\t    void close();\n    58\t}\n===== KeyStoreKeyProvider.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.security.crypto;\n     2\t\n     3\timport javax.crypto.SecretKey;\n     4\timport java.io.InputStream;\n     5\timport java.nio.file.Files;\n     6\timport java.nio.file.Path;\n     7\timport java.security.KeyStore;\n     8\timport java.util.Enumeration;\n     9\timport java.util.Objects;\n    10\timport 14\t\n    15\t/**\n    16\t * KeyProvider implementation that securely loads symmetric AES-256 keys\n    17\t * from a standard Java KeyStore (PKCS12 format).\n    18\t * <p>\n    19\t * Satisfies:\n    20\t * <ul>\n    21\t *   <li>Rule 1 (Trust Boundaries): Validates file paths, permissions, and key aliases.</li>\n    22\t *   <li>Rule 2 (Secrets): Clears char array passwords from memory *   <li>Rule 8 (Prefer the Platform): Utilizes standard Java PKCS12 KeyStore platform mechanisms.</li>\n    26\t * </ul>\n    27\t */\n    28\tpublic final class KeyStoreKeyProvider implements KeyProvider {\n    29\t\n    30\t    private static final String DEFAULT_KEYSTORE_TYPE = \"PKCS12\";\n    31\t    private static final String REQUIRED_ALGORITHM = \"AES\";\n    32\t    private static final int REQUIRED_KEY_LENGTH_BYTES = 32;\n    33\t\n    34\t    private final ConcurrentHashMap<String, SecretKey> keys = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n    35\t    private final AtomicReference<String> activeKeyId = new AtomicReference<>(null);\n    36\t    private final AtomicBoolean closed = new AtomicBoolean(false);\n    37\t\n    38\t    /**\n    39\t     * Loads a KeyStore from a secure filesystem path.\n    40\t     *\n    41\t     * @param keyStorePath     The path to the KeyStore file.\n    42\t     * @param password         The KeyStore password as a char array (will be zeroed upon return).\n    43\t     * @param activeKeyAlias   The alias of the key to use as the active encryption key.\n    44\t     * @return Initialized KeyStoreKeyProvider.\n    45\t     */\n    46\t    public static KeyStoreKeyProvider fromFile(Path keyStorePath, char[] password, String activeKeyAlias) {\n    47\t        Path validatedPath = FileSecurityUtil.validatePrivateFile(keyStorePath);\n    48\t        try (InputStream in = Files.newInputStream(validatedPath)) {\n    49\t            return fromInputStream(in, password, activeKeyAlias);\n    50\t        } catch (Exception e) {\n    51\t            if (e instanceof CryptoException ce) {\n    52\t                throw ce;\n    53\t            }\n    54\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Failed to load KeyStore from file\", e);\n    55\t        }\n    56\t    }\n    57\t\n    58\t    /**\n    59\t     * Loads a KeyStore from an input stream.\n    60\t     *\n    61\t     * @param inputStream      Stream containing the KeyStore key.\n    64\t     * @return Initialized KeyStoreKeyProvider.\n    65\t     */\n    66\t    public static KeyStoreKeyProvider fromInputStream(InputStream inputStream, char[] password, String activeKeyAlias) {\n    67\t        Objects.requireNonNull(inputStream, \"InputStream must not be null\");\n    68\t        Objects.requireNonNull(password, \"Password must not be null\");\n    69\t        Objects.requireNonNull(activeKeyAlias, \"Active key alias must not be null\");\n    70\t\n    71\t        KeyStoreKeyProvider provider = new KeyStoreKeyProvider();\n    72\t        try {\n    73\t            KeyStore keyStore = KeyStore.getInstance(DEFAULT_KEYSTORE_TYPE);\n    74\t            keyStore.load(inputStream, password);\n    75\t\n    76\t            KeyStore.PasswordProtection protParam = new KeyStore.PasswordProtection(password);\n    77\t            Enumeration<String> aliases = keyStore.aliases();\n    78\t            while (aliases.hasMoreElements()) {\n    79\t                String alias = aliases.nextElement();\n    80\t                if (keyStore.isKeyEntry(alias)) {\n    81\t                    KeyStore.Entry entry = keyStore.getEntry(alias, protParam);\n    82\t                    if (entry instanceof KeyStore.SecretKeyEntry secretEntry) {\n    83\t                        SecretKey key = secretEntry.getSecretKey();\n    84\t                        if (REQUIRED_ALGORITHM.equalsIgnoreCase(key.getAlgorithm())) {\n    85\t                            byte[] encoded = key.getEncoded();\n    86\t                            if (encoded != null) {\n    87\t                                try {\n    88\t                                    if (encoded.length == REQUIRED_KEY_LENGTH_BYTES) {\n    89\t                                        provider.keys.put(alias, key);\n    90\t                                    }\n    91\t                                } finally {\n    92\t                                    CryptoUtils.wipe(encoded);\n    93\t                                }\n    94\t                            }\n    95\t                        }\n    96\t                    }\n    97\t                }\n    98\t            }\n    99\t\n   100\t            // Verify active key alias exists\n   101\t            CryptoUtils.validateKeyId(activeKeyAlias);\n   102\t            if (!provider.keys.containsKey(activeKeyAlias)) {\n   103\t                provider.close();\n   104\t                throw new CryptoException(\"Active key alias '\" + activeKeyAlias + \"' not found in KeyStore\");\n   105\t            }\n   106\t            provider.activeKeyId.set(activeKeyAlias);\n   107\t\n   108\t            return provider;\n   109\t        } catch (CryptoException ce) {\n   110\t            provider.close();\n   111\t            throw ce;\n   112\t        } catch (Exception e) {\n   113\t            provider.close();\n   114\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Failed to initialize KeyStore: invalid format or credentials\", e);\n   115\t        } finally {\n   116\t            CryptoUtils.wipe(password);\n   117\t        }\n   118\t    }\n   119\t\n   120\t    private void ensureOpen() {\n   121\t        if (closed.get()) {\n   122\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Key provider has been closed and destroyed\");\n   123\t        }\n   124\t    }\n   125\t\n   126\t    @Override\n   127\t    public SecretKey getActiveKey() {\n   128\t        ensureOpen();\n   129\t        String currentActiveId = activeKeyId.get();\n   130\t        if (currentActiveId == null) {\n   131\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Invalid cryptographic request: no active key configured\");\n   132\t        }\n   133\t        SecretKey key = keys.get(currentActiveId);\n   134\t        if (key == null) {\n   135\t            throw new CryptoException(\"Invalid cryptographic request: active key not found in key provider\");\n   136\t 147\t        return currentActiveId;\n   148\t    }\n   149\t\n   150\t    @Override\n   151\t    public Optional<SecretKey> getKey(String keyId) {\n   152\t        ensureOpen();\n   153\t        if (keyId == null) {\n   154\t            return Optional.empty();\n   155\t        }\n   156\t        return Optional.ofNullable(keys.get(keyId));\n   157\t    }\n   158\t\n   159\t    @Override\n keys.containsKey(keyId);\n   166\t    }\n   167\t\n   168\t    @Override\n   169\t    public void close() {\n   170\t        if (closed.compareAndSet(false, true)) {\n   171\t     }\n   177\t    }\n   178\t\n   179\t    @Override\n   180\t    public String toString() {\n   181\t        return \"KeyStoreKeyProvider[closed=\" + closed.get()\n   182\t                + \", Use when deploying or configuring encryption for data at rest, establishing controls to meet compliance requirements, or reviewing an implementation during a security assessment.\ndomain: cybersecurity\nsubdomain: cryptography\ntags:\n- cryptography\n- encryption\n- aes\n- data-at-rest\n- symmetric-encryption\nversion: '1.0'\nauthor: mahipal\nlicense: Apache-2.0\nnist_csf:\n- PR.DS-01\n- PR.DS-02\n- PR.DS-10\nmitre_attack:\n- T1600\n- T1573\n- T1553\n- T1486\n---\n# Implementing AES Encryption for Data at Rest\n\n## Overview\n\nAES (Advanced Encryption Standard) is a symmetric block cipher standardized by NIST (FIPS 197) used to protect classified and sensitive data. This skill covers implementing AES-256 encryption in GCM mode for encrypting files and data stores at rest, including proper key derivation, IV/nonce management, and authenticated encryption.\n\n\n## When to Use\n\n- When deploying or configuring implementing aes encryption for data at rest capabilities in your environment\n- When establishing security controls aligned to compliance requirements\n- When building or improving security architecture for this domain\n- When conducting security assessments that require this implementation\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- Familiarity with cryptography concepts and tools\n- Access to a test or lab environment for safe execution\n- Python 3.8+ with required dependencies installed\n- Appropriate authorization for any testing activities\n\n## Objectives\n\n- Implement AES-256-GCM encryption and decryption for files\n- Derive encryption keys from passwords using PBKDF2 and Argon2\n- Manage initialization vectors (IVs) and nonces securely\n- Encrypt and decrypt entire directory trees\n- Implement authenticated encryption to detect tampering\n- Handle large files with streaming encryption\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n### AES Modes of Operation\n\n| Mode | Authentication | Parallelizable | Use Case |\n|------|---------------|----------------|----------|\n| GCM  | Yes (AEAD)    | Yes            | Network data, file encryption |\n| CBC  | No            | Decrypt only   | Legacy systems, disk encryption |\n| CTR  | No            | Yes            | Streaming encryption |\n| CCM  | Yes (AEAD)    | No             | IoT, constrained environments |\n\n### Key Derivation\n\nNever use raw passwords as encryption keys. Always derive keys using:\n- **PBKDF2**: NIST-approved, widely supported (minimum 600,000 iterations as of 2024)\n- **Argon2id**: Winner of Password Hashing Competition, memory-hard\n- **scrypt**: Memory-hard, good alternative to Argon2\n\n### Nonce/IV Management\n\n- GCM requires a 96-bit (12-byte) nonce that must NEVER be reused with the same key\n- Generate nonces using `os.urandom()` (CSPRNG)\n- Store nonce alongside ciphertext (it is not secret)\n\n## Workflow\n\n1. Install the `cryptography` library: `pip install cryptography`\n2. Generate or derive an encryption key\n3. Create a random nonce for each encryption operation\n4. Encrypt data using AES-256-GCM with the key and nonce\n5. Store nonce + ciphertext + authentication tag together\n6. For decryption, extract nonce, verify tag, and decrypt\n\n## Encrypted File Format\n\n```\n[salt: 16 bytes][nonce: 12 bytes][ciphertext: variable][tag: 16 bytes]\n```\n\n## Security Considerations\n\n- Always use authenticated encryption (GCM, CCM) to prevent tampering\n- Never reuse a nonce with the same key (catastrophic in GCM)\n- Use at least 256-bit keys for long-term data protection\n- Securely wipe keys from memory after use when possible\n- Rotate encryption keys periodically per organizational policy\n- For disk-level encryption, consider XTS mode (AES-XTS)\n\n## Validation Criteria\n\n- [ ] AES-256-GCM encryption produces valid ciphertext\n- [ ] Decryption recovers original plaintext exactly\n- [ ] Authentication tag detects any ciphertext modification\n- [ ] Key derivation uses sufficient iterations/parameters\n- [ ] Nonces are never reused for the same key\n- [ ] Large files (>1GB) can be processed via streaming\n- [ ] Encrypted file format includes all necessary metadata\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}
